Running an exercise safely

  • Use test accounts. Every account on this install is a test account of one person (see test accounts). Several exercises need roles no account holds yet (production_manager, operator, qp): make them with E1 first, with a username that says it is a test account, for example qp_test, and add each one to the test accounts table.
  • Give each account its own browser session. All the web apps share one sign-in. If you sign in as a second account in another tab of the same window, the first account is replaced everywhere, and the screens then act for whoever signed in last. Use a normal window for one account, a private window for a second, and another browser or browser profile for a third. Private windows of the same browser share one session with each other, so two private windows are still one account.
  • Every exercise leaves permanent records. Approvals, returns, decisions, releases and the policy history can be added to but never changed or deleted; the policy history is hash-chained, so a removed or edited event would show. That is what the exercises are meant to prove. Never try to clean up a record afterwards. An approved test item can be retired, a test account deactivated, a unit removed; each of those is a new record too.
  • Real equipment is real. A unit in test mode drives its chamber. Do not send commands to a unit with plants in it unless that is the point of the exercise.

Where to read the evidence

By decision, nobody has an SQL console on production data (see who reaches production data). Everything an exercise records is read on these screens and in these files:

WhereWho can read itWhat it shows
Configuration UI, Approvals tab, Approval historyqa, qp, auditorThe newest 100 approval records, filtered by All, Recipes, Control algorithms, Parameter sets, Settings and commissioning, Master data and device reviews, Run releases or Q-table retirements: time, item, action, person with role and source, authors, note. History of this item in a review shows one item's records.
Configuration UI, Approvals tab, My proposals and draftsEvery signed-in person, for their own workWhere each of your proposals and drafts stands, with a return's reason.
Configuration UI, Units tab, History in the Commissioning cellqa, qp, auditorEvery commissioning proposal for the unit, with proposer, value, reason and outcome.
User Manager, Decisions tab (Waiting for qa, To review, All)admin and auditor read; qa checksEvery account decision: maker, subject, change, reason, checker, times and note.
User Manager, Requests, Access review, Change logAs in User ManagerAccess requests and their history, the quarterly reviews, mail settings and template changes.
Scheduler and Run MonitorThe roles that read runsRuns, their status and cycles. The Released badge, with who and when, is shown in the Scheduler only (Completed Runs).
./health-check.sh on the hostThe host operatoriampending (a configuration change waiting for qa over 7 days), iamdecisions (an account decision waiting over 7 days), iamchain (the policy history's hash chain verifies). See the health check.
logs/ on the hostThe platform user on the hostEvery command writes logs/<name>-<timestamp>.log, for example health-check-… and app-iam-units-…. The first line of a command's output names its log.
Database Management exportqp and auditor, for the groups their role may export (qa holds X on no group and cannot export)The rows themselves, as a file, when a screen is not enough. Every export is in the export log.

The policy history has no screen of its own: iamchain checks that it verifies, and the access review shows its verdict.

How every exercise is written

Each exercise has the same parts, in this order:

  1. Goal: what you learn and what the exercise proves.
  2. Accounts and roles: which accounts take part, and which separation of duties the exercise shows.
  3. Before you start: what must be true first.
  4. Steps: numbered; who does what, with the screen, tab, button and field names as the page shows them.
  5. What you should see: the result on the screen.
  6. What is recorded and where to check it: the records, and the screen or file that shows them.
  7. Refusals worth trying: what the platform must refuse, and the message it shows.
  8. Afterwards: how to leave the platform as you found it.
  9. Last done: the date and result, or "not yet".

E1 Request access and grant a role

Goal. Make a new account the way every person gets one, and see that an approved request gives no access until a second person confirms it.

Accounts and roles. A new applicant (no account yet), admin (admin) and svieira (qa). The administrator approves; a qa person who is neither the administrator nor the applicant confirms. Rule I17: no person may hold an admin and a qa account at once (see dual control).

Before you start. A username and an e-mail address that have no account yet. If e-mail is not set up in the User Manager, no e-mail goes out; the decision is still recorded.

Steps.

  1. Applicant, in a window of its own: open https://access.<domain>/register. The sign-in page opens on the Request access tab. Fill in every field, choose the Role you need and a Business justification (20 characters or more), and choose Submit request.
  2. Applicant: try to sign in with the new username and password.
  3. admin: User Manager, Requests, tab Pending. Select the request. Under Decision choose Approve, check Role to grant, write Notes, untick Send e-mail to if the address is not real, click Approve as <role> (waits for qa) and confirm the dialog.
  4. admin: open Decisions and select the new decision.
  5. qa: User Manager, Decisions, tab Waiting for qa. Select the decision. Read the effect, the access it adds and the list of existing admin and qa accounts. Write an optional note and click Confirm.
  6. Applicant: sign in again.

What you should see. After step 1: "Request received. You will get an e-mail once an administrator has reviewed it." In step 2: "This account is not active". After step 3 the request reads approved, but the account stays disabled with no role. In step 4 the administrator reads the decision but cannot check it. After step 6 the applicant signs in with the granted role.

The request and its history (submitted, approved, reassigned if the role changed) are under Requests, tab All. The grant is one account decision naming the maker, the checker, both times and the notes: Decisions, tab All. The confirmation is also appended to the hash-chained policy history (iamchain). iamdecisions warns if a grant waits more than 7 days.

Refusals worth trying.

  • admin opens the waiting decision (step 4): it shows no Confirm, Refuse or Mark reviewed, because only qa holds A on account decisions. The administrator never confirms.
  • A qa person opens a decision they made, or one about their own account: "You cannot check this decision:" followed by the reason, for example "not_own_author: you are its subject". The rule compares persons, not account names.
  • Submitting a second request with the same e-mail address while the first waits, or with a username that already has an account: the form refuses it.
  • qa clicks Refuse with an empty note: "A refusal needs a note."
  • I17: giving a qa account the person_id of an admin account, or the reverse, is refused before anything is recorded, with "person … would hold both an admin account (…) and a qa account (…) (I17)".

Afterwards. The account stays. Add it to the test accounts table. When it is no longer needed, deactivate it (E7); it is never deleted. A refused grant deletes the pending account, so the applicant may apply again.

Last done. Not recorded here.

E2 Assign a unit to an operator

Goal. Give an operator a unit and see that the operator sees nothing of it until qa confirms.

Accounts and roles. The person who runs units.sh on the host (recorded as maker with --by), a qa person who did not run it (svieira), and an operator account (from E1). The script cannot prove who ran it, so qa attests that they did not.

Before you start. An enabled operator account; the unit exists in the Units tab; a shell on the platform host in the repository folder. units.sh takes the deploy lock, so no deploy may be running.

Steps.

  1. Operator: open the Scheduler and the Run Monitor. Note what you see.
  2. Host: run ./apps/iam/units.sh assign <operator username> UNIT01 --by <your username>. For --by, name an account that is not the qa person who will confirm.
  3. Host: run ./apps/iam/units.sh list. The unit is listed under WAITING_QA.
  4. Operator: reload the Scheduler. Still nothing.
  5. qa: User Manager, Decisions, Waiting for qa. Select the unit assignment. Read the note that units.sh recorded its maker but cannot verify it. Tick "I did not run units.sh for this assignment.", write an optional note and click Confirm.
  6. Host: ./apps/iam/units.sh list again. Operator: reload the Scheduler and the Run Monitor.

What you should see. Before the confirmation the operator sees no runs and no telemetry and can start nothing. After it, the operator sees UNIT01's runs, cycles and logs in the Scheduler and its telemetry in the Run Monitor, and list shows the unit under UNITS.

One account decision per unit, with the maker as "system:deploy (by <username>)", the checker, the note with the attestation, and the times: Decisions, tab All. The unit taking effect appends unit_assigned to the policy history, with the qa person as actor (iamchain). The command's output is in logs/app-iam-units-<timestamp>.log.

Refusals worth trying.

  • Confirm stays off until the attestation box is ticked.
  • Run the assignment with --by naming the qa account, then open it as that account: "You cannot check this decision:" with the reason that you made it. Refuse it from another qa account or remove it with units.sh remove.
  • units.sh assign without --by is a usage error (exit 2) and records nothing.

Afterwards. Keep the assignment if the operator is needed for E6. Otherwise run ./apps/iam/units.sh remove <operator username> UNIT01 --by <your username>: a removal acts at once and qa marks it reviewed under To review. See unit assignments.

Last done. 31 October 2026: an assignment of UNIT01 confirmed by svieira at 22:13 (decision 14). The operator's view (steps 1, 4 and 6) was not checked: no operator account was enabled.

E3 Draft and approve a parameter set

Goal. Make a parameter set the way every configuration item is made: an engineer drafts, a different person approves, and the approved set is frozen.

Accounts and roles. eng (engineer) writes the draft; svieira (qa) approves. The approval is refused when the approver is the same person as anyone who changed the item since its last approval.

Before you start. The algorithm the set is for is approved (its status shows under its code in Control Algorithms). The database enforces roles (IAM_DB_ROLES=true), so the Approvals tab is shown.

Steps.

  1. engineer: Configuration UI, Control Models, panel Parameter Sets, + New Parameter Set. Enter the Name, choose the Algorithm and the Unit, adjust Params (JSON object) and click Save draft.
  2. engineer: open the Approvals tab. The draft is under My proposals and drafts, waiting for qa.
  3. qa: Approvals, panel Waiting for approval, Review… on the parameter set. Read What you approve and the list of changes (for a new set: Never approved: N change(s) since creation). Type a Note and click Approve.
  4. engineer: open the set again in Parameter Sets.

What you should see. After step 3 the message names the approval record ("Approved: … (approval #N)"). The set's status reads approved; it opens read-only with ⎘ Copy to a new draft and Retire…. A schedule can now choose it.

Every save is stamped with who made it and kept in the row-change log. The approval is a row naming the approver, role and source, the authors, a digest of the content and the note: Approval history, filter Parameter sets, or History of this item.

Refusals worth trying.

  • The author cannot approve. With separate roles, eng has no Approve at all and sees the draft only under My proposals and drafts. The person rule shows when the approver's account and an author's carry the same person_id: "You changed this item (or one of its steps) since its last approval. Another qa person must approve it." (database code not_own_author).
  • An approved set cannot be edited: the screen offers no Edit, and the database refuses an edit from any other client with draft_only: … is approved, not a draft; a change starts a new draft. Use ⎘ Copy to a new draft.
  • A set whose algorithm is still a draft cannot be approved: the row says "Approve its control algorithm first." and Approve is off. This can be tried only while some algorithm is a draft.

Afterwards. The approved set stays. If it should not be used, Retire… it (it asks you to confirm; nothing is deleted).

Last done. 31 October 2026: q_test (#1), created and edited by eng, approved by svieira at 21:59:54 UTC (approval #6).

E4 Return and withdraw

Goal. Send work back with a reason, change it and send it again; and take back a proposal of your own.

Accounts and roles. eng (engineer) as author and proposer; svieira (qa) returns. Only qa returns; only the proposer withdraws.

Before you start. A draft parameter set by eng (as in E3, up to step 2). For the withdrawal, a unit with no run running or scheduled.

Steps.

  1. qa: Approvals, Review… on the draft. Type the reason in the note field and click Return…, then confirm.
  2. engineer: Approvals, My proposals and drafts. The draft reads Returned by qa, with qa's reason, and "Change the draft to send it back".
  3. engineer: Control Models, Parameter Sets, Edit on the draft, change it, Save draft.
  4. qa: the draft is back in Waiting for approval and may be approved.
  5. engineer: Units tab, Flag for commissioning… on the unit, a reason, Propose. Then Withdraw… (in the unit's cell or under My proposals and drafts), with an optional reason.

What you should see. After step 1: "Returned: … (record #N)"; the draft stays an editable draft, marked returned in the queue until its author changes it. After step 5 the proposal reads withdrawn and never applies.

A return and a withdrawal are rows in the approval record (actions return and withdraw) with the reason; no approval rule counts them as approvals. Read them in Approval history (filter Parameter sets for the return, Settings and commissioning for the withdrawal) and the unit's History.

Refusals worth trying.

  • A draft returned and not changed since cannot be returned again: Return… is not offered, and the database refuses it with "Already returned; it has not changed since."
  • Someone else's proposal cannot be withdrawn: another engineer's proposal shows without buttons, and the database refuses with "Only the person who proposed it can withdraw it." This needs a second engineer account.
  • qa clicks Return… with an empty note: "Give the reason for returning it in the note field".

Afterwards. Approve or retire the test draft. A withdrawn proposal is closed; nothing to undo.

Last done. Not yet on production; only probed on the throwaway test database.

E5 Test mode and the MQTT Explorer

Goal. Put a unit in test mode (commissioning), send a command from the MQTT Explorer, and end test mode, seeing the broker follow each change.

Accounts and roles. eng (engineer) proposes, sends and ends; svieira (qa) approves and reviews. Turning test mode on opens command rights, so it waits for qa; ending it narrows them, so it acts at once and is reviewed afterwards.

Before you start. The unit (here UNIT01) has no run running or scheduled; its Commissioning cell reads Off; IAM_MQTT_PERSON=true. If the MQTT Explorer says "The MQTT Explorer needs IAM_MQTT_PERSON=true", the switch is off (a fresh install starts with it off): since 1 November 2026 there is no shared account to fall back on, so the host operator turns it on first with ./guide.sh (IAM switches).

A message on a control topic reaches the unit's CONTROL board. Use a tag that no channel in the board's channel map uses, such as test, unless you mean to switch equipment.

Steps.

  1. engineer: Configuration UI, Units, in UNIT01's Commissioning cell Flag for commissioning…. Type the Reason and click Propose.
  2. qa: Approvals, Review… on the proposal. Read the proposer, time and reason, type an optional note and click Approve and apply.
  3. engineer: open the MQTT Explorer. Wait until the status reads Connected (up to 15 seconds for a new credential). Read Your rights: its topics are patterns.
  4. engineer: in the Publish box type one concrete topic, medcea/UNIT01/ZONE01/control/test, a payload, and click Send.
  5. engineer: send the same to a unit that is not in test mode, for example medcea/UNIT02/ZONE01/control/test.
  6. engineer: Units, End commissioning…, a reason, End commissioning. After about 15 seconds send step 4 again.
  7. qa: Approvals, Review… on the ended commissioning, Mark reviewed.

What you should see. After step 1 the cell reads "Waiting for qa: flag on … (your proposal)". After step 2 it reads Commissioning and the message names the approval record. In step 4 the message arrives on that topic. In step 5 nothing happens and no error is shown: the broker drops a message you may not send. Step 6 applies at once; the cell reads Off with an end waiting for review, and the resend in step 6 is dropped in the same silent way.

Each proposal is a pending change with the proposer and reason; each approval, review and withdrawal a row in the approval record: Approval history, filter Settings and commissioning, and the unit's History. The policy history gets commissioning_enabled and commissioning_disabled with the unit, actor and reason, and mqtt_credential_issued for the engineer's credential (iamchain). iampending warns if a proposal or an end waits for qa more than 7 days.

Refusals worth trying.

  • A topic with + or #, such as the pattern medcea/+/+/control/# copied from Your rights: "Not sent: + and # only match topics; send to one, e.g. medcea/UNIT01/ZONE01/control/<tag>".
  • While the unit has a run running or scheduled, Flag for commissioning… is off, with "Run #N is running" (or "is scheduled"); the database refuses the proposal and the approval too. The other way round, no run can be scheduled on a flagged unit.
  • A second proposal for a unit whose proposal already waits: refused until it is approved, returned or withdrawn.
  • qa cannot propose test mode: there is no Flag for commissioning… for them; only an engineer proposes. In the Units cell, Review… is off for the proposer's own change.

Afterwards. Every unit's cell reads Off, nothing waits in Waiting for approval, and ./health-check.sh passes iampending and iamchain. See commissioning a unit and MQTT Explorer.

Last done. 31 October 2026: two full cycles on UNIT01 (pending changes #1 to #4, approvals #7 and #8 for the first cycle, the second approved at 22:40:18 and reviewed at 22:43:18, policy history events commissioning_enabled and commissioning_disabled); sending from the Explorer verified. The same evening found that a wildcard topic made the broker close the Explorer's connection; fixed that day (see known gaps).

E6 Release a completed run

Goal. Take a run from preparation to release, and see that the release can only come from a person who had no hand in the run.

Accounts and roles. A production_manager prepares the run; the same production manager, or an operator assigned to the unit, starts it; a qp person who neither created, changed nor executed it releases it. None of these roles is held by an account on this install yet: make them with E1, and assign the operator with E2.

Before you start.

  • An approved recipe allowed on UNIT01 in Recipe ↔ Unit Permissions. A short one (one step, one cycle of a few minutes) keeps the exercise short; eng drafts it and svieira approves it as in E3.
  • An approved parameter set for the unit, for example q_test.
  • UNIT01 is not in test mode and has no active run.

Steps.

  1. production_manager: Configuration UI, Units, Recipe ↔ Unit Permissions. If the recipe has no tick for UNIT01, click the circle, choose the Default Algorithm and click Save.
  2. production_manager: Scheduler, New Schedule. Choose UNIT01 and the recipe, Frequency Run Once with a time (UTC), the approved Parameter set, and click Save.
  3. Let the schedule start the run, or the production manager clicks ▶ Run Now on it in Scheduled Jobs. An operator of UNIT01 may click ▶ Execute on a Queued run the production manager prepared.
  4. Follow it in Current Runs or the Run Monitor until it is Completed.
  5. qp: Configuration UI, Approvals, Review… on the Completed run row "Run #<id> (<recipe> on UNIT01)". Read What you release: recipe and parameter set with their approvals, creator, executors, attempts, cycles, start and end, and whether you may release it.
  6. qp: type an optional Release note, click Release and confirm.
  7. Any role that reads runs: Scheduler, Completed Runs.

What you should see. The confirmation says a release is final. The message reads "Released: … (approval #N)" and the row leaves the queue. In the Scheduler the run shows a Released badge; hovering over it shows who released it and when (UTC).

The run records who created and who executed it, with their roles. The release is a run-release row (one per run, never changed) and an approval row with action release, the note, and the persons who created, changed or executed the run: Approval history, filter Run releases. See releasing a completed run.

Refusals worth trying.

  • A person who created, changed (for example rescheduled) or executed the run cannot release it: "You created, changed or executed this run (one of its attempts). Another qp person must release it." With separate accounts per role this shows only when the qp account carries the same person_id as one of them.
  • A run with no recorded person behind its creation or start is listed with "No recorded person created or started this run: …" and is never released.
  • A run already released is not offered again; if it was released meanwhile: "Already released meanwhile; the list was refreshed."
  • qp clicks View Log on the run in the Scheduler: "(your role does not hold R on run logs)". The release is decided on the summary, not the log.
  • Setting a released run back for a retry is refused.

Afterwards. Nothing to undo: a release is final. Retire the Run Once schedule if it is still listed.

Last done. Not yet. It needs a completed run and a qp account.

E7 Deactivate and reactivate an account

Goal. Take an account out of use and bring it back, and see that removing access acts at once while giving it back waits for a second person.

Accounts and roles. admin (admin) deactivates and proposes the activation; svieira (qa) reviews the deactivation and confirms the activation. The subject is a test account that is not needed for anything else.

Before you start. The account is enabled and is not the last enabled admin or qa. For an operator, remove its units first with units.sh remove if they should not come back with it.

Steps.

  1. admin: User Manager, Users, tab Active. Select the account, write the Reason and click Deactivate account.
  2. Subject: try to keep working, then sign in again.
  3. qa: Decisions, tab To review. Select the deactivation and click Mark reviewed.
  4. admin: Users, tab Inactive. Select the account and click Activate (waits for qa).
  5. qa: Decisions, Waiting for qa, select the activation, Confirm.

What you should see. Step 1 applies at once: the person is signed out everywhere and their MQTT credentials are revoked; data, role and history are kept. In step 2 sign-in shows "This account is not active". After step 4 the account is still disabled; after step 5 it signs in again with its role.

Both changes are account decisions with maker, subject, reason, checker and note: Decisions, tab All. Status changes are kept in the append-only account status history; the review and the confirmation are appended to the policy history (iamchain).

Refusals worth trying.

  • The administrator selects their own account: "This is your own account — another administrator would have to change it."
  • The administrator deactivates svieira, today the only enabled qa account: refused with "this is the last enabled qa person", before anything is recorded. The same holds for the last enabled administrator.
  • While an activation waits for qa, the account's role and person cannot be changed until it is decided or withdrawn with Withdraw decision.

Afterwards. Leave the account as it should be: reactivated if still needed, otherwise deactivated. See when your account is deactivated.

Last done. 31 October 2026: developer deactivated as a test of the retire path, after its units were removed with units.sh (decision 12). Reactivation: not yet.

E8 Quarterly access review

Goal. Review who holds which role and unit, sign it off, and read it as an auditor.

Accounts and roles. svieira (qa) creates and signs off; viewer (auditor) reads. One qa person may create and sign off the same review: a known single-person control.

Before you start. The tab holds one review per quarter, and its snapshot never changes. Creating it is this quarter's review, not practice: run this exercise when the review is due.

Steps.

  1. qa: User Manager, Access review, Create this quarter's review.
  2. qa: read the snapshot: accounts with role, state, person_id and last login; units; Unexplained at the time of the review; decisions waiting and made since the last review; waivers; the IAM switches; the policy version and hash-chain verdict. Compare the accounts with the test accounts table.
  3. qa: write an optional note and click Sign off.
  4. auditor: User Manager, Access review.

What you should see. The signed-off review with its snapshot, note and signer. The auditor reads it but has no create or sign-off button.

The review, its snapshot and its sign-off are kept in the User Manager's Access review tab, readable by qa, qp and auditor. See the quarterly access review.

Refusals worth trying.

  • auditor and qp cannot create or sign off a review.
  • A signed-off review cannot be undone or changed.

Signing off a review that lists unexplained roles accepts them: the drift banner in the Decisions tab stops showing them until a later change to that account. Explain or correct them first.

Afterwards. Nothing to undo.

Last done. Not recorded here.

E9 Open an example notebook in a new workbench home

Goal. Start an AI Workbench workspace for the first time and open an example notebook at once, without a restart. The exercise proves that the workspace's installs finish before it starts (before 1 November 2026 a new home folder opened no document until its workspace was restarted) and that the workspace shows the person: the terminal prompt and the git author name are the person's username, not the container's account jovyan.

Accounts and roles. A test account that has never started a workspace, holding a role the workbench admits, for example a new bio_test (bio_scientist) made with E1 and added to the test accounts table. admin (admin, hub administrator) watches from the hub's admin page and can stop the workspace afterwards; the administrator cannot open it.

Before you start. ./infra/deploy-workbench.sh (or ./deploy-infra.sh) has run since 1 November 2026, and ./health-check.sh shows iamworkbench OK. The account must be new to the workbench: a home folder already set up by an earlier start proves nothing about a first start. Use a browser session of its own (see running an exercise safely).

Steps.

  1. Test account: on the portal, click the JupyterHub card and sign in.
  2. Wait on the start page until JupyterLab opens. The first start takes a few minutes.
  3. In the file browser, open medcea-examples, then 01-connect.ipynb.
  4. Open a terminal (File → New → Terminal), read the prompt, then run git config --global user.name.
  5. admin: on the hub's admin page, find the test account's workspace and its start time.

What you should see. In step 3 the notebook opens with its cells; it never stays on "The document is taking some time to load". In step 4 the prompt reads bio_test@workbench:~$, the terminal tab's title names bio_test, and git config --global user.name prints bio_test. The workspace's log lists its set-up steps before the notebook server starts.

Nothing is recorded in the platform's database: the exercise changes no right and no record. Evidence is the workspace's log on the host, which the host operator reads: kubectl -n medcea get pods -l component=singleuser-server names the workspace's pod (jupyter-…, the username with special characters escaped), then kubectl -n medcea logs <pod> | grep -E "medcea-home-setup|is running at". Every medcea-home-setup: line, ending with medcea-home-setup: done, comes before the line saying the Jupyter Server is running, and no line holds WARNING. The hub's admin page shows when the workspace started.

Refusals worth trying.

  • admin: follow the link to the test account's workspace from the admin page. The hub answers "403 Forbidden"; the administrator cannot open the workspace or its notebooks.
  • Test account: in the terminal, run touch ~/medcea-examples/x. It answers touch: cannot touch '/home/jovyan/medcea-examples/x': Read-only file system, because the folder is mounted read-only. Then change a cell of 01-connect.ipynb and save it (Ctrl+S). The save is refused: JupyterLab may show a "File Save Error for 01-connect.ipynb" dialog naming the read-only file system or "Permission denied", or, with real-time collaboration on, show nothing at all. Either way, close the notebook and open it again: your change is gone. Copy the notebook into your home folder to change it.

Afterwards. Stop the workspace (File → Hub Control Panel, Stop My Server, or admin from the admin page). The home folder is kept. Deactivate the test account if it is not needed again (E7).

Last done. Not yet.

E10 Sign out and switch accounts

Goal. Sign out as one account and sign in as another in the same browser window, and see each app follow the new person. The exercise proves the decision of 1 November 2026 ("short sessions and one-hop sign-out"). Sign out ends the app it is chosen in, the platform sign-in and the AI Workbench's own session. The AI Workbench follows the person signed in now within 5 minutes, and so does a Grafana tab that is not in use. Nobody is shown another person's workspace. Before that day the workbench still served the previous person after a sign-out.

Accounts and roles. Two test accounts used one after the other in one browser window. Account A is eng (engineer), which may open the AI Workbench, Grafana (Editor) and the Node-RED editor. Account B is admin (admin), which may open the AI Workbench and pgAdmin, but not Grafana or the Node-RED editor. The exercise shows that two people never share an identity through one browser.

Before you start. ./deploy-infra.sh, then ./apps/access/deploy.sh, then ./health-check.sh have run, in that order, since the review of 1 November 2026, and the AI Workbench is installed (WORKBENCH_ENABLED=true). Contrary to the usual advice (see running an exercise safely), use one private window for both accounts. Sharing one window is the point of this exercise. Do not tick Remember me. Have a watch at hand.

Steps.

  1. A: in a new private window, open the portal and sign in as eng.
  2. A: open the AI Workbench from the portal and wait until JupyterLab shows. Note the address, …/user/eng/lab. Leave the tab open.
  3. A: open Grafana from the portal and open the IoT Environmental Monitoring dashboard. Grafana's profile menu (top right) names eng. Set auto-refresh to Off next to the time range, so the tab is not in use. Leave the tab open and do not touch it.
  4. A: open the Node-RED editor from the portal. Leave the tab open.
  5. A: on the portal tab, open the user menu (your name, top right) and choose Sign out. Note the time.
  6. A: on the page "Do you want to log out?", choose Logout.
  7. B: on the sign-in page you land on, sign in as admin.
  8. B: open the AI Workbench from the portal.
  9. B: open pgAdmin from the Platform Management page.
  10. B: in the tab of step 2, try to save the notebook or open a file, then reload the tab.
  11. B: when at least 5 minutes have passed since step 3, reload the Grafana tab of step 3.
  12. B: then, at least 5 minutes after step 6, reload the Node-RED tab of step 4.

What you should see.

  • Step 5 shows "Do you want to log out?" (the portal has signed you out of itself first).
  • Step 6 passes through the AI Workbench's address and lands on the portal's sign-in page.
  • Step 8 opens …/user/admin/lab. File → Hub Control Panel names admin, and nothing shows eng.
  • Step 9 goes to pgAdmin's browser tree without a MedCEA sign-in button.
  • In step 10, before the reload, the save may still work: the old tab keeps eng's token for about 5 to 10 minutes. After the reload the workbench answers "403 Forbidden" for eng's workspace.
  • In step 11 the Grafana tab goes through the sign-in and shows Grafana's sign-in page saying the login was denied, because admin has no Grafana role. It does not show eng's dashboards as eng.
  • Step 12 shows the page "No access to Node-RED".

Nothing is recorded in the platform's database: the exercise changes no right and no record. The host operator can read the evidence:

  • The hub's log has a line User logged out: eng at the time of step 6, if step 6 came within 5 minutes of step 2. Read it with kubectl -n medcea logs deploy/hub | grep "logged out".
  • In the Keycloak admin console, Users → eng → Sessions lists no session after step 6.
  • Grafana's log has a refused sign-in for admin after step 11.

Write the times of steps 5, 6 and 11 under Last done.

Refusals worth trying.

  • B: type the address of A's workspace (https://ai-workbench.<domain>/user/eng/lab). The workbench answers "403 Forbidden".
  • A, in a second run: on the "Do you want to log out?" page, close the tab instead of confirming. The portal signed A out of itself, but the platform sign-in stays: opening the portal signs A in again without a password, and the AI Workbench still shows A. This is why the page must be confirmed.

Known gaps you may see (see switching accounts in one browser):

  • Reloading the Node-RED tab less than 4 minutes after step 6 may still open the editor as eng: its gate checks the platform sign-in every 4 minutes. If B were another engineer, whom the gate admits, the editor would keep acting as eng for up to an hour after step 4.
  • pgAdmin opened by A, if A could open it, would stay A's for up to 60 minutes without use, and at most a day.
  • A Grafana tab that stays in use, for example a dashboard with auto-refresh, keeps eng's Grafana session for up to an hour after step 3, so in step 11 it may still show eng. The hour is the bound; 5 minutes would drop unsaved panel edits.
  • If the AI Workbench does not answer, step 6 stops on its error page. The platform sign-in has already ended; only the workbench's own session is left, for up to 5 minutes.

Afterwards. B: Sign out, then close the private window. Stop both workspaces if they are not needed (admin, from the hub's admin page).

Last done. 1 November 2026, on the workbench, with the test accounts svieira and admin (one person; read from the hub's log): 17:44:22 svieira signed in to the AI Workbench; 17:44:56 Sign out passed through the workbench's own logout and on to the platform sign-out; 17:45:10 admin signed in and the workbench opened /user/admin/, not the previous person's server. Observed: when the workbench is still open in another tab while "Do you want to log out?" waits, that tab signs the same person in to the workbench again before the confirmation (17:45:28, 0.1 s later); that workbench session then ends within 5 minutes of the confirmation. Close other workbench tabs before signing out. Grafana and pgAdmin were not checked in this run.

E11 Keep an Editor's dashboard across a restart

Goal. Make a dashboard of your own in Grafana, restart Grafana, and find it still there. The exercise proves the decision of 1 November 2026 ("Grafana keeps its data"): Grafana keeps its database in its own volume, so an Editor's dashboards and an open session survive a restart, while the platform's dashboards still change only through the repository. Before that day an Editor's dashboard vanished at the next restart.

Accounts and roles. eng (engineer, Grafana Editor) makes the dashboard. A qa test account (Grafana Viewer) looks at it. The host operator restarts Grafana.

Before you start. ./deploy-infra.sh and ./apps/report/deploy.sh have run since 1 November 2026, and ./health-check.sh shows Grafana volume OK. Use one browser session per account (see running an exercise safely).

Steps.

  1. eng: open Grafana from the portal, then Dashboards → New → New dashboard, Add visualization, choose the PostgreSQL data source, and save the dashboard as E11 <date>.
  2. eng: open IoT Environmental Monitoring, change a panel's title and try to save. Then use Save as and save the copy as E11 copy <date>.
  3. qa: open Grafana and search for E11.
  4. Host operator: microk8s kubectl -n medcea rollout restart deployment/grafana, then microk8s kubectl -n medcea rollout status deployment/grafana. Note the time.
  5. eng, within 5 minutes of step 2: reload the Grafana tab, then search for E11.

What you should see.

  • Step 1 saves the new dashboard.
  • In step 2 the plain save of the platform dashboard is refused: Grafana says the dashboard cannot be saved from the Grafana UI because it has been provisioned from another source, and offers its JSON instead. Save as works.
  • Step 3 lists both E11 dashboards for the qa account, which can open them but has no Edit or Save.
  • In step 4 Grafana is unavailable for a few seconds while the old pod stops before the new one starts (one Grafana at a time on the volume). Afterwards ./health-check.sh shows Grafana volume OK with root mode 700: the volume's directory is Grafana's alone.
  • In step 5 the tab reloads without the platform sign-in (the session survived the restart), the profile menu still names eng, and both E11 dashboards are listed. IoT Environmental Monitoring shows its original panel title.

Nothing is recorded in the platform's database: Grafana's own dashboards are not part of the platform's record. Grafana keeps who saved each version: open the dashboard, Dashboard settings → Versions names eng and the time. The host operator reads the restart with microk8s kubectl -n medcea get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=grafana (the pod's age) and the volume with microk8s kubectl -n medcea get pvc grafana (Bound). Write the times of steps 1, 4 and 5 under Last done.

Refusals worth trying.

  • qa: open Dashboards; there is no New button, and Explore is not in the menu.
  • eng: in the copy from step 2, open Dashboard settings → JSON Model, change the title and save. It works: an Editor's own dashboard is the Editor's to change. Only the platform's dashboards are refused.

Afterwards. eng: delete both E11 dashboards (Dashboard settings → Delete dashboard). A dashboard that should stay in the platform goes into the repository instead (Grafana).

Last done. Not yet.

E12 Make and check a backup

Goal. Make a backup on demand, check it, and bring back a file a person deleted from their AI workbench home. The exercise proves the decision of 1 November 2026 ("Nightly backups to a local folder"): the backup holds the person's home, its checksums show it is unchanged, only the host account can read it, and the documented restore gives the file back. (E11 is the Grafana exercise of the same day, so this one is E12.)

Accounts and roles. The host operator (the account that ran ./apps/backup/deploy.sh; on this install svieira, also the platform admin) makes, checks and restores the backup. A bio_scientist test account (any role the AI Workbench admits will do) creates and deletes the file. On this one-person install both are the same human (see test accounts).

Before you start. ./apps/backup/deploy.sh has run (./health-check.sh shows Backups as OK or, on its first day, SKIP), and the test account has started its AI workbench at least once, so its home claim-<name> exists. Use one browser session for the test account (see running an exercise safely).

Steps.

  1. Test account: open the AI Workbench from the portal, then File → New → Text File, type one line, and save it as e12-<date>.txt in the home folder.
  2. Host operator: ./guide.sh → Backups → Make a backup now (or ./apps/backup/backups.sh now), answer y. Note the folder name the log prints after backup: folder, and the time.
  3. Host operator: Backups → List the backups, then Backups → Check a backup's checksums with that folder name.
  4. Host operator: ./health-check.sh.
  5. Test account: delete e12-<date>.txt (right-click → Delete), then stop the workspace: File → Hub Control Panel → Stop My Server.
  6. Host operator: Backups → How to restore with the folder name, and follow part C for one file: find D, then tar -tzf F/volumes/claim-<name>.tar.gz | grep e12, then sudo tar -C "$D" -xzpf F/volumes/claim-<name>.tar.gz --numeric-owner ./e12-<date>.txt.
  7. Test account: sign in, open the AI Workbench, and open e12-<date>.txt.

What you should see.

  • Step 2 prints the Job's log: pg_dump of medcea_db and keycloak, one tar line per workbench home, node-red and grafana, then OK: … complete, Job … succeeded and the list of folders. No password appears.
  • Step 3 lists the folder as complete with its size, and the check ends OK: <folder>, N files match the checksums written at backup time.
  • Step 4 shows Backups OK (with -v: newest complete backup <folder>, 0 h old).
  • Step 6 lists exactly one ./e12-<date>.txt in the archive and restores it without an error.
  • In step 7 the file is back with the line typed in step 1.

Nothing is recorded in the platform's database: a backup and a restore are host operations. The evidence is the folder itself (MANIFEST with every file's size and SHA-256, OK with the finish time), the Job (microk8s kubectl -n medcea get jobs -l app=medcea-backup, named medcea-backup-now-<time>), and the logs logs/backups-now-<time>.log and logs/backups-verify-<time>.log. Write the folder name and the times of steps 2 and 7 under Last done.

Refusals worth trying.

  • Host operator: sudo -u nobody ls /var/backups/medcea is refused with "Permission denied": only the deploying account (and root) can read the backups.
  • Host operator: ./apps/backup/backups.sh verify ../etc is refused: "'../etc' is not a backup folder name (like 20261102-023000Z)." The guide does not accept such a name at all.
  • Host operator, only if list shows a folder as incomplete (a run that failed): verify on it is refused: "has no OK marker: that run did not finish; choose a complete one." Never change a real backup to provoke a checksum failure; tests/test_backup.py covers that offline.
  • Host operator: ./apps/backup/backups.sh delete is refused ("Unknown command"): nothing in the platform deletes a backup on request; only the nightly run deletes folders older than BACKUP_KEEP_DAYS.

Afterwards. Test account: delete e12-<date>.txt again. The backup folder from step 2 stays; it is deleted with the others once it is older than BACKUP_KEEP_DAYS.

Last done. 2 November 2026, on the install upgraded in place to v0.4.0 (read from logs/): the scheduled run at 02:30 UTC completed (folder 20261102-023001Z); ./apps/backup/backups.sh now at 08:10 completed 20261102-081015Z (dumps of medcea_db and keycloak, the roles without passwords, and the volumes claim-admin, claim-svieira, grafana and node-red; 891 MB, 7 files); verify matched all 7 checksums; the folder and every file are readable by the platform account only (700/600); the health check's Backups line turned OK (52 of 52). The backups are on the same disk as the data (the deploy warns): a restore was not tried.

E13 Version 1 and the skeleton gate

Goal. See version 1 of the access policy appear on the record without any change to anyone's access, and see the skeleton gate refuse a change in git that would give access. The exercise proves the decisions of 2 November 2026 "Version 1 of the access policy" and "Break-glass and the skeleton gate" (see the decision record).

Accounts and roles. The host operator (the platform admin, who runs the deploys). No other account takes part; version 1's confirmation by a qa person comes with the Policy UI (IAM 4.3).

Before you start. IAM 4.1 is deployed and slice 4.2a is pulled; git status -- apps/iam shows nothing; ./guide.sh → Policy → Policy versions: status shows state: empty and skeleton: none accepted yet; no other deploy and no recipe run is active.

Steps.

  1. ./guide.sh → Policy → Deploy IAM 4.2a, with your own username as --by, and answer y.
  2. Policy → Policy versions: status.
  3. ./health-check.sh.
  4. In the repository, add one route to the skeleton that roles already holding R on its group would reach: in apps/iam/policy/defaults.yaml, under resource_groups: → audit_histories, add "GET /api/e13-probe": R to the routes of the member {type: api, service: iam, …}. Then run ./apps/iam/deploy.sh. (Do not edit matrix: for this: a matrix edit in git is not refused but ignored, since the matrix comes from the applied version; that is what "git may lag" reports.)
  5. git checkout -- apps/iam/policy/defaults.yaml, then ./apps/iam/deploy.sh.

What you should see.

  • Step 1: the database deploy, ./deploy-infra.sh, then the iam deploy printing Policy versions..., the bootstrap ("version 1 compiles to exactly what defaults.yaml compiles to (… targets): nothing is re-rendered"), [policy] state/policy-applied.yaml: version 1 (bootstrap) written, the grants check OK, the report lines, and "version 1 (bootstrap) applied: nothing was re-rendered".
  • Step 2: applied: version 1 (bootstrap), claimed by person <your person_id>, waiting for qa (4.3); skeleton: accepted … , git …: equal; host file: … matches the database; bootstrap_applied among the last policy events.
  • Step 3: 54 checks (55 since the Platform manual check of 3 Nov 2026); Access policy version OK ("version 1 (bootstrap), qa confirms it in the Policy UI once it exists (IAM 4.3)"); Policy artefacts OK.
  • Step 4: the deploy stops before compiling anything: "ERROR: this git change gives access that has not been approved …", one line per role that gains the route (auditor, qa, qp: "1 new: route iam GET /api/e13-probe"), "It cannot deploy until policy approvals can be applied (IAM 4.5) …", "Break-glass (MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE) does not bypass this check …", "Nothing was changed."
  • Step 5: a normal deploy.

The policy history holds, for version 1: skeleton_accepted, skeleton_synced, draft_created, status_changed, apply_started and bootstrap_applied, and account_seeded for the qa and engineer accounts of the install (observed, from step 1's ./deploy-infra.sh). The refusal of step 4 is in the deploy's log (logs/app-iam-deploy-<time>.log, or the guide's log), and nothing about it is in the history. Until the Policy UI's History tab exists (IAM 4.3), read the events with ./apps/iam/apply.sh --status. Write the event numbers it shows and the time of step 4 under Last done.

Refusals worth trying.

  • ./apps/iam/apply.sh --bootstrap --by <you> again: "policy versions exist: --bootstrap is only for an empty iam_policy.version".
  • ./apps/iam/apply.sh 1 --by <you>: "apply.sh <version> arrives with IAM 4.5".
  • With step 4's edit in place: MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE=apps/iam/policy/defaults.yaml MEDCEA_POLICY_BREAK_GLASS="E13 test" ./apps/iam/deploy.sh: the same refusal as step 4. Break-glass does not bypass the gate, and nothing is recorded.
  • MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE=x ./apps/iam/deploy.sh without a reason: "MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE is a break-glass override: set MEDCEA_POLICY_BREAK_GLASS=<reason>, or unset MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE".
  • Instead of step 4's edit, add - {service: monitor, route: "GET /api/e13-public", why: E13} under public_routes: and run ./apps/iam/deploy.sh: the same refusal, listed as "anonymous and services: public monitor GET /api/e13-public" (no role gains it; the gate still counts a route anyone may call). Revert it as in step 5. Both edits were checked offline with cli.py skeleton-gate --against-git HEAD --check when this exercise was written, so the checker's own rules do not refuse them first.
  • Do not edit state/policy-applied.yaml: tests/test_policy_source.py proves offline that every compile refuses an edited one.

Afterwards. git status shows apps/iam/policy/defaults.yaml unchanged. Version 1 and its events stay: they are the platform's permanent record.

Last done. Steps 1 and 3 on 3 Nov 2026, by the live deploy: version 1 (bootstrap) applied at 09:59 UTC, claimed by svieira; events 42–46 (skeleton_accepted to status_changed) and 48 (bootstrap_applied), account_seeded 36 and 37 from ./deploy-infra.sh at 09:36; host file matches the database; health 54/54 at 10:13. The first attempt at 09:38 was refused inside the bundle and rolled back (nothing changed; fixed in 2dc8422). Steps 2, 4 and 5 and the refusals: not yet.

E16 Confirm version 1 in the Access Policy

Goal. Read the applied access policy and its history in the browser, and confirm version 1 as a qa person who did not apply it, through that person's own sign-in. The exercise proves the decision of 3 November 2026 "Confirming version 1" (see the decision record).

Accounts and roles. A qa account whose person_id is not the one apply.sh recorded (on this install sergio_qa; activated through the User Manager, an account decision a qa person confirms); the account named by apply.sh --bootstrap --by (on this install svieira, also qa and QA_USER) for the refusals; an operator account (for example operator_test, see E1). See the test accounts: on this install they belong to one human.

Before you start. Slice 4.3 is deployed (IAM 4.3); ./health-check.sh shows Access policy version WARN "version 1 (bootstrap) is not confirmed by qa yet"; IAM_POSTGREST_JWT and IAM_DB_ROLES are true. Give each account its own browser session.

Steps.

  1. qa: open the portal and click Access Policy. The page opens on the Confirm tab.
  2. qa: open Matrix; point at a role and a group name; click the cell qa × Access policy.
  3. qa: open History.
  4. qa: back in Confirm, tick "I did not run apply.sh for this version.", write the note "E16: read the matrix and the history; this is the factory policy", press Confirm.
  5. Host operator: ./health-check.sh and ./apps/iam/apply.sh --status.

What you should see.

  • Step 1: version 1 (bootstrap, applied), its time, its reason and the claimed person.
  • Step 2: the checker accepts the version, with its two waiver notes. The names show their explanations. The cell panel shows levels R and A, A only if not_own_author with its meaning, and what only this cell gives: among others "database: EXECUTE on medcea.confirm_policy(integer,text,text)".
  • Step 3: "Record intact", "The version audit finds nothing", and the events of version 1 down to skeleton_accepted.
  • Step 4: "Version 1 confirmed. It is in the history." The Confirm tab then says "Nothing waits for confirmation", and History shows a new confirmed event by the qa account.
  • Step 5: Access policy version OK "version 1 (bootstrap), skeleton …, host file matches"; --status shows confirmed with the time.

The policy history holds confirmed for version 1 with the qa person as actor and the note; iam_policy.version carries confirmed_at, confirmed_by_person and the note, and iam_policy.approval_evidence the token's evidence. The confirmation is permanent. Write the event number and the time under Last done.

Refusals worth trying (before step 4, or with a later bootstrap or rollback version):

  • As the account apply.sh recorded (svieira): the card says "(you)" next to the claimed person and "You applied this version …, so you cannot confirm it", and it offers no statement and no Confirm button (since 4 November 2026). A confirmation sent anyway is refused by the database, "You applied version 1 yourself (apply.sh recorded your person). Another quality assurance person confirms it.", and nothing is written. Before that change the page still offered Confirm; on 4 November 2026, 10:35–10:37 UTC, five attempts as svieira were refused this way (iam-api log; config-api 42501), and the history stayed at event 48.
  • Confirm without ticking the statement, or without a note: the buttons stay disabled.
  • An operator opening https://policy.<domain>: the sign-in proxy's page "No access to Access Policy".
  • Do not try Dispute… on version 1: a dispute is permanent and the health check fails until a later version supersedes it. tests/test_iam_api.py proves the dispute path offline.

Afterwards. Nothing to undo: the confirmation is the record.

Last done. 4 November 2026. Refusal: six confirmations as svieira, the account apply.sh --bootstrap --by named (10:35–10:59 UTC), all refused 403 "You applied version 1 yourself"; nothing written, the history stayed at event 48. Confirmation: sergio_qa (activated through the User Manager, confirmed by svieira) confirmed version 1 at 11:10:59 UTC, history event 51 confirmed, version 1, actor sergio_qa, person 96b98720…; iam_policy.version carries confirmed_at and the person; one approval_evidence row; verify_chain() ok. ./health-check.sh 11:15 UTC: Access policy version OK; ./apps/iam/apply.sh --status: "confirmed 2026-11-04T11:10:59". The note was a placeholder rather than the step's text. Not tried: the operator refusal and the unticked-statement state.

E17 Change the access policy (to approved, then withdrawn)

Goal. Draft a small change to the access policy as an admin, submit it, and have a qa person who is none of its authors approve it on the diff the page shows; then withdraw it, since applying arrives with slice 4.5. The exercise proves the decisions of 4 November 2026 "The approval diff" and "A version that widens the approver's own role" (see the decision record).

Accounts and roles. Maker: admin (admin, the install's ADMIN_USER). Approver: sergio_qa (qa). For the refusals: svieira (qa). Before you start, check in the User Manager that sergio_qa's and svieira's person_ids differ from admin's. See the test accounts: on this install they belong to one human.

Before you start. Slice 4.4 is deployed (IAM 4.4); ./apps/iam/apply.sh --status shows no open version; IAM_POSTGREST_JWT and IAM_DB_ROLES are true; no policy break-glass is open. Give each account its own browser session.

Steps.

  1. admin: Access Policy → Changes → New draft from the applied version, reason "E17: remove a level nobody needs", Create the draft.
  2. admin: in Matrix, click a cell that holds a level a role does not need for its work (a narrowing, so no qa role is widened), untick that level, write a reason, Save the cell.
  3. admin: in Changes, Submit for approval, with a reason.
  4. qa (sergio_qa): Approval, open the version, read the diff, write a note, Approve.
  5. admin: Changes → Withdraw… on the approved version, reason "E17 done: applying arrives with 4.5".
  6. Host operator: ./apps/iam/apply.sh --status and ./health-check.sh.

What you should see.

  • Step 1: a new version, kind change, status draft, with you as maker; the checker's verdict is green.
  • Step 2: the cell shows the new levels; the checker stays green (or names the rule the change breaks: then choose another cell).
  • Step 3: status submitted; the version is no longer editable.
  • Step 4: the diff lists the changed cell (before → after) and what the role loses; no "This version widens a role you hold"; then "Version N approved. It is applied when the host operator runs apply.sh (IAM 4.5)."
  • Step 5: status withdrawn.
  • Step 6: no open version; between steps 4 and 5 the health check WARNs "version N (change) is approved; applying it arrives with IAM 4.5", after step 5 it is green again.

The policy history holds draft_created, two entry_saved (one at creation, recording the checker's verdict), submitted, approved (with the three digests and the token's hash), withdrawn and a status_changed per step; iam_policy.approval_evidence holds the approval's signed token. Read them in History (as qa). All permanent. Write the version and event numbers under Last done.

Refusals worth trying.

  • As admin, approving: the Approval tab is not offered, and the route answers 403.
  • Submitting a draft the checker refuses (save a cell that breaks a rule): Submit for approval stays disabled; sent anyway, 409, and the page says "Version N fails the policy's checker now: fix the errors it lists first."
  • Approving from a page opened before a later save (qa returns it, admin saves a cell and submits again, then approve from the old page): refused, "Version N changed since you opened it. Reload the page."
  • As svieira (qa), approving once it is approved: refused, "Version N is not waiting for approval (any more). Reload the page." (the database's "version N is not submitted").

Afterwards. Nothing to undo: the version is withdrawn and stays in the record. Never leave an approved version open.

Last done. 4 November 2026, on production after the IAM 4.4 deploy (commit 73cd61e). admin drafted version 5 from applied version 1 at 21:33:19 (history events 94–96, the third the creation verdict on auditor / audit_histories, saved unchanged), set engineer on master_data from RCU to RC with the checker green (event 97) and submitted it at 21:52:37 (event 99). The approval was made by svieira (qa), not sergio_qa as planned: still an eligible approver, since its person_id (36bd0bd4…) is none of the authors' (admin, a68681f5…); approved at 21:53:23 (event 101, with the policy token's evidence; diff e5349a8496f8…). admin withdrew it at 21:55:53 (event 103); afterwards no open version, the record verifies (89 events), the health check 54 OK and 0 failures. The reasons and notes typed were placeholders. The refusals were not tried on this run; the live test tests/iam-policy.sh made the admin, author and wrong-diff refusals the same evening (version 4, all 13 steps passed).

E18 Propose a git skeleton change and approve it

Goal. Take a git change that gives access (which every deploy refuses) through the only path it has: a skeleton version proposed from its commit, approved by a qa person with the attestation. The exercise proves the decision of 4 November 2026 "Who made a skeleton version" (see the decision record).

Accounts and roles. The host operator (on this install the OS account svieira, which the version's reason records), naming admin with --by; approver sergio_qa (qa), whose person_id differs from admin's. On this install they belong to one human.

Before you start. As E17; no open version. Choose a harmless widening that no qa or auditor role gains, for example one more read route for engineer in a group it already reads.

Steps.

  1. Host operator: on a branch, change apps/iam/policy/defaults.yaml and commit; then go back to the deploying branch (HEAD at the accepted skeleton).
  2. Host operator: ./apps/iam/apply.sh --propose-skeleton --by admin --reason "E18: one read route" --from-git <commit> --check-only, then the same without --check-only, answering y (or ./guide.sh → Policy → Propose a git skeleton change for approval).
  3. qa: Access Policy → Approval, open the skeleton version, read the header and the diff, tick "I did not run apply.sh --propose-skeleton for this version.", write a note, Approve.
  4. Host operator: ./apps/iam/apply.sh --withdraw-open --by admin --reason "E18 done: applying arrives with 4.5" (or Withdraw… in the Access Policy as admin).
  5. Host operator: ./apps/iam/apply.sh --status, ./health-check.sh.

What you should see.

  • Step 2: what engineer gains, the digests, and "version N (skeleton) submitted: a qa person other than admin, who did not run this proposal, approves it in the Policy UI (Approval) …".
  • Step 3: the note reads "A git change: commit …, proposed by the host operator for person … (a claim of apply.sh --by; the reason names the username)"; the diff lists the route under engineer and the skeleton sections that change; Approve is enabled only with the box ticked and a note.
  • Steps 4 and 5: "version N withdrawn (system:apply)"; no open version; the health check is green.

submitted (kind skeleton, with the commit, the claimed --by person and the reason naming the username and OS account), approved (the note begins with the attestation), withdrawn (by system:apply); the stored diff in iam_policy.version_diff; the proposed skeleton stays unaccepted. All permanent.

Refusals worth trying.

  • Approving without ticking the attestation: Approve stays disabled; sent anyway, refused: "Tick the statement that you did not run apply.sh --propose-skeleton for this version."
  • Approving as the --by person (propose with --by svieira, a qa account, and approve as svieira): refused, "You are an author of version N: another quality assurance person approves it."
  • ./deploy-infra.sh with the branch checked out: refused by the skeleton gate before anything changes, approved version or not.

Afterwards. Keep the branch or delete it; the version is withdrawn and stays in the record.

Last done. 4 November 2026, on production. The branch e18/one-read-route (commit fa81d0c) adds GET /_mqtt-credential/status (R) to the MQTT Explorer group, which only engineer holds. The host operator (OS account svieira) ran --check-only, then proposed it --by admin at 21:58:03: version 6, kind skeleton, skeleton 5c579f9cc14e over the accepted b02325cd0f52, adding only that route for engineer (history events 104–105, the reason naming --by admin (claimed) and the OS user). sergio_qa approved it at 22:01:58 with the attestation (event 107; diff 024b616e366e…, approved on skeleton 5c579f9cc14e). --withdraw-open --by admin withdrew it at 22:02:27 as system:apply (event 109); afterwards no open version, the accepted skeleton still equal to git, the record verifies, the health check 0 failures. The note typed after the attestation was a placeholder. The refusals were not tried on this run.

Adding an exercise

Every change a user would notice gets an exercise in this chapter, in the same piece of work as the change: a new screen, button, rule or refusal. Extend an exercise when the change belongs to it; add a new one (E9, E10 and so on) when it does not.

  1. Write it in the form of How every exercise is written, all nine parts. Take the screen, tab, button and field names and the refusal texts from the code, not from memory.
  2. Add its row to Exercises at a glance: exercise, roles, what it proves, last done ("Not yet").
  3. After the change is deployed, run the exercise on the platform.
  4. Write the date and the result under Last done and in the summary row, with the record numbers the screens showed (approval, decision, pending change). Never write a number you did not see.
  5. When the exercise is evidence that a control works, record it in the decision record as well, and list anything it found in known gaps.

Reference

Lookup tables for everyday use: the web addresses and who may open them, the roles each application admits, the access levels and resource groups of the access policy, the settings an administrator may change, the scripts on the platform host, the health checks, and the abbreviations used in this manual. The tables describe the platform as of IAM 2.6, deployed report-only on 31 October 2026, with the access policy at its default version.

Web addresses

Every web application has its own address, <name>.$DOMAIN, set by a *_DOMAIN setting in config.env. Every address except the access request form signs you in through Keycloak first. "Who can open it" is the list python3 apps/iam/compiler/cli.py hosts prints from the access policy. Where the gate is not compiled from the policy yet, the last column says what applies today.

The access policy also names an Access Policy host (POLICY_DOMAIN, for admin, qa, qp and auditor). It is not deployed yet (IAM Phase 4) and has no setting in config.env.example. With IAM_GATES="false" the compiled hosts fall back to the pre-policy list operator, qa, auditor, admin. Sensor and control boards do not use a web address: they reach the MQTT broker on port 31883 of the platform host.

For device onboarding, maintenance may only re-onboard into a slot that already exists. In Grafana, engineer is Editor and the other roles marked "yes" are Viewer. In the Monitor, the two scientist roles read telemetry but not runs. Roles qc and qp are defined for later use.

Opening an application is only the first gate. What you may do on the page is decided by your access levels on each resource group, checked by the service and by the database.

Access levels

Every cell of the access policy holds zero or more of these levels. A level that "includes" others gives them too.

LetterNameIncludesMeaning
NNoneNo access. The role does not see the resource at all. The default for every cell.
RReadSee the resource, change nothing.
XRead and exportRRead, and take a copy out of the platform through the Database Management export. Each export is recorded with who exported which objects and when.
CCreateRAdd new records; never change existing ones. Used for appending to regulated data and audit trails.
UUpdateC, RCreate and change records, within the group's constraint. On recipes and controllers only while the record is a draft. Removing a configuration record means setting it to "retired", never deleting it. Never given to a person on regulated or history data.
AApproveRApprove or release another person's work. The approver must be a different person from the author. In this version an approval is an attributable, time-stamped record, not a 21 CFR Part 11 electronic signature.
OOperateRStart, stop or trigger a process (execute a run, stop it, trigger a schedule) without changing its definition.
KConfigureAdminister the tool without the content it holds. On accounts it means reading and changing account records, which are personal data. Held only by admin.
DDeleteRemove platform components and releases on the host. Never data. Held only by admin.

Resource groups

Each row of the access policy is a resource group: tables, service routes, web pages and MQTT topics that belong together. The class decides the rules that always apply to the group.

regulatedRegulated dataRecords produced while making product. Created once, never changed or deleted by anyone; corrections are new records.
historyAudit historyAppend-only trails of who did what, including approval records and export logs. Written by the system only; readable by reviewers.
personalPersonal dataAccounts, names, e-mail addresses, unit assignments and access decisions. Administered by admin, confirmed by qa, readable by reviewers.
configConfigurationMaster data, recipes, controllers, schedules, settings, dashboards and tools. Changed under the group's approval rule.

Access policy versions (IAM 4.1 to 4.4)

Since slice 4.1 the database holds everything Phase 4 needs to keep the access policy as numbered versions (migration 008c). Since slice 4.2a version 1 exists (the factory policy, applied by ./apps/iam/apply.sh --bootstrap inside the iam deploy), every deploy compiles from the applied version, and every deploy runs the skeleton gate (see The applied access policy). Since slice 4.3 the Access Policy shows the versions, and since slice 4.4 an admin drafts change versions there and a qa person approves them; applying an approved version is slice 4.5.

ObjectWhat it is
iam_policy.versionOne row per version: kind (bootstrap, change, skeleton, rollback), status (draft, submitted, approved, applied, superseded, withdrawn), who drafted, submitted, approved, applied, confirmed or disputed it, and its digests. At most one change or skeleton version is open at a time (one_open).
iam_policy.entryThe matrix of a version, one row per role, group and level, with the constraint that narrows that level.
iam_policy.waiverThe version's waivers, with the objects each one covers.
iam_policy.skeletonPlatform skeletons (everything of the policy but the matrix and waivers) by digest; the one this host accepted last is in force.
iam_policy.version_diffFor a skeleton version, the diff the host computed, shown to qa byte for byte.
iam_policy.approval_evidenceThe signed token behind every approve, return, confirm and dispute. Readable by the database owner only.

The six tables are written only by the policy's own database functions. A trigger refuses anything else, for every role, the database owner included: no update without the function's mark, no delete (except replacing a cell of a draft), no truncate, one step of the state machine per update, and every status change written to the policy history (status_changed).

FunctionWho may call itRefusals
iam_policy.new_draft, save_entry, submit, withdrawiam-api (iam_svc) for a signed-in admin (C or U on iam_policy)Not the Policy UI's own person, or a blank person_id: refused. Access changed since the person signed in: "your access changed since you signed in: reload the page". No applied version, or one being applied: refused. Another version open: refused.
medcea.approve_policy, return_policy, confirm_policy, dispute_policyA qa person through the configuration API (PostgREST), with their own token of the policy hostAny other sign-in: "approve access-policy versions in the Policy UI". An author of the version (by account or person): refused. A stale base, changed entries, or another platform skeleton than this host accepted: refused, reload. The person apply.sh recorded may not confirm. Since 4.4 an approval is also refused while a policy break-glass is open, and for a skeleton version without the attestation, when it was proposed against an older accepted skeleton, or when it widens the approver's role.
iam_policy.why_not, entries_digest, audit_versionsRoles that read iam_policy (and iam-api, and Database Management's account)Read only.
iam._changed_sinceiam-apiRead only: whether a person's access changed since their token was issued. Installed by ./apps/access/deploy.sh.
iam_policy.append_historyiam-api (since 4.2a only mqtt_credential_issued), and the platform's own scripts as postgresAny event a policy version is read from (draft_created, submitted, approved, applied, apply_started, status_changed, break_glass_ended, every name starting apply_, skeleton_, waiver_, break_glass_, bootstrap_ or policy_, in any letter case): "is a policy version event, written only by the policy functions". From iam-api's account any other event than mqtt_credential_issued: "append_history: iam_svc writes only mqtt_credential_issued". A version in the payload is dropped. So whoever holds iam-api's database password cannot record a version as applied, approved or authored.
iam_policy._system_as (4.2a)The policy's own functions (database owner only)Sets the system actor of the transaction when none is set, accepts one of the allowed ones, and refuses any other: "the policy is built and applied by …, not by …". apply.sh acts as system:apply; a deploy's skeleton gate and its records as system:deploy. Accepting and syncing a skeleton allow both; building, applying and reverting a version only system:apply.
iam_policy._record (4.2a rules)The scripts as postgres, through apply.sh and dba_record_eventapplied, partial_apply, bootstrap_applied and apply_deferred: only system:apply, with a version number. break_glass_seen, break_glass_ended and account_seeded: system:apply or system:deploy; a missing version is filled in with the applied version. A policy break-glass (source: file) needs a version and a reason. An account_seeded needs username, sub, fact and a fact_id equal to the hash of its content (refused otherwise); the same content is recorded once.

The configuration API accepts tokens of the policy host's client (policy) for exactly these four calls, by POST, and nothing else: no read and no other write. The network entry is network.clients.iam-approvals with only_rpc in the policy. A qa token of the Configuration UI cannot approve a policy version either.

The route table (routes.json) gains dual_control: the roles that may approve and the roles that make changes on iam_policy and on iam_users (today qa and admin for both). The User Manager and apply.sh read it from IAM 4.5.

Since 4.2a the route table's digest no longer counts its source (the name of the policy it was compiled from) or applied_version: both are still in routes.json, but the same policy gives the same digest whatever it was read from. The digest moved once, at the first ./deploy-infra.sh of slice 4.2a. The configuration API's admission of the policy host's client is checked whether IAM_DB_ROLES is true or false.

Policy events of slices 4.2a and 4.4

EventWritten byPayload
bootstrap_appliedapply.sh --bootstrap, after its transaction (and --resume)version 1, kind bootstrap, os_user, claimed_by_sub and claimed_by_person (the --by account), git_commit, skeleton_digest, compiler_digest, artefacts (the digest of every compiled output), changed and deferred (empty), apply_log (the log's path), chain_head_before (the id and hash of the history's last event before the apply).
account_seeded./deploy-infra.sh (directly, or queued in state/seed-facts.jsonl and recorded by the next deploy)fact (created, person_id_set or observed), username, sub, role, person_id, by, os_user, at, fact_id (the SHA-256 of the fact's content), from_queue when it waited in the queue. No e-mail address or password.
break_glass_seen (source: file)A deploy run with MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE, before its first compileversion, file_sha256, snapshot (state/break-glass/…), skeleton_digest, entries_digest, reason, os_user, entry_script, components, lock_holder. (Node-RED's editor break-glass, recorded by ./deploy-infra.sh, is the same event without source: file.)
break_glass_endedThe next normal deploy of each component named in a policy break-glassversion, since_event, component, os_user, entry_script.
skeleton_acceptedA deploy (narrowing) or apply.shSince 4.2a also from: the digest of the skeleton it replaced, so the health check can recompute every accept.

entry_saved (4.4)An admin (save_entry); once also at a draft's creation, with before = after, to record the checker's verdictversion, role, group, before and after ({level: constraint or null}), verdict_ok; the reason.
