submitted (4.4, skeleton)apply.sh --propose-skeleton as system:applyversion, kind skeleton, skeleton_digest (the proposed one), diff_digest, commit, claimed_by (sub, person of --by), entries_digest; the reason, ending "[apply.sh --propose-skeleton, --by <username> (claimed), OS user <account>]".
withdrawn (4.4)An admin (withdraw), or apply.sh --withdraw-openversion; from the host also by system:apply, and the reason ends with the --by username and OS user.
approved (4.4)A qa person through the configuration APIversion, source, role, person, sub, the token's iat, jti and azp, entries_digest, diff_digest, skeleton_digest, jws_sha256 (the stored token's hash); the note (for a skeleton version beginning with the attestation).
returned (4.4)A qa person through the configuration APIversion, source, role, person, sub, iat, jti, azp, jws_sha256; the note.

Each status change also writes status_changed. Approve and return store the signed token in iam_policy.approval_evidence.

The Policy UI's routes (iam-api port 8083)

Behind iam-oauth2-proxy at https://policy.<domain> (Keycloak client policy). Every route is checked against the compiled route table whatever IAM_ENFORCE says; /healthz answers before the check. Writes need Content-Type: application/json (415) and the page's own Origin (403).

RouteGroup, levelRolesWhat it does
GET /, GET /api/mepolicy_page Radmin, auditor, qa, qpThe page; who you are, which tabs and actions you have, your role's closure.
GET /api/policyiam_policy Radmin, auditor, qaEvery version, the open one with its authors, the accepted skeleton, whether you may approve.
GET /api/policy/<v>, …/<v>/explainiam_policy Rthe sameA version composed with the skeleton, the checker's verdict; one cell explained.
GET /api/policy/<v>/diff?against=appliediam_policy Rthe sameThe approval diff (below) of a change or skeleton version; 409 for other kinds.
GET /api/policy/who-can, /service-identities, /break-glassiam_policy Rthe sameThe Who can…, Service identities and Break-glass tabs.
GET /api/policy/history, /chainaudit_histories Rauditor, qa, qpThe policy events (at most 500) and the version audit; whether the hash chain is intact.
POST /api/policy/draftiam_policy Cadmin{from, reason}: a new draft, with the checker's verdict recorded. 201.
PUT /api/policy/<v>/entryiam_policy Uadmin{role, group, levels: {L: constraint or null}, reason}: replaces the whole cell.
POST /api/policy/<v>/submit, …/withdrawiam_policy Uadmin{reason}.
POST /api/policy/<v>/approveiam_policy Aqa{note, entries_digest, diff_digest, skeleton_digest, attest}; checked by iam-api, then forwarded with the person's token to /rpc/approve_policy.
POST /api/policy/<v>/return, …/confirm, …/disputeiam_policy Aqa{note} (and the attestation for confirm); forwarded the same way.

The approval diff

What a qa person approves. For a change iam-api computes it from the applied version and the submitted one, both with the skeleton it ships; for a skeleton version apply.sh --propose-skeleton computes it on the host and the database stores it (iam_policy.version_diff).

{"schema": 1, "kind": "change"|"skeleton", "version": N, "against": <applied version>,
 "entries_digest": "<64 hex>",
 "skeleton": null | {"from": "<accepted digest>", "to": "<proposed digest>", "sections": [...]},
 "cells":   [{"group": g, "role": r, "before": {L: constraint|null}, "after": {...}}, ...],
 "waivers": {"added": [...], "removed": [...]},
 "roles":   {role: {"added": [tuple, ...], "removed": [tuple, ...]}, ...},
 "system":  {"added": [...], "removed": [...]}}

Only cells that differ are listed, sorted by the bytes of group and role; roles lists only roles whose reach changes; a tuple is one thing a role or service can reach (a database privilege, a route, a host, an MQTT topic, a tool setting, a level). The document carries no time, commit or compiler version. Its canonical form is JSON with sorted keys, no spaces (, and : as separators) and non-ASCII characters written as themselves, encoded as UTF-8; diff_digest is the SHA-256 of those bytes in lower-case hex. iam-api serves exactly those bytes with the headers X-Medcea-Skeleton-Digest and X-Medcea-Diff-Digest, and the browser hashes what it received before showing it.

The host policy file

state/policy-applied.yaml (mode 600 in a mode-700 state/, not in git, written only by the scripts under the deploy lock):

# medcea-applied: version=N kind=bootstrap|change|skeleton|rollback entries_digest=<64 hex> skeleton_digest=<64 hex> git_commit=<40 hex>[-dirty]|unknown written_at=<UTC>
# medcea-applied-body: body_digest=<64 hex> groups=<n> roles=<n> dropped=<n> keys=<64 hex>
# Written by infra/lib-policy-source.sh (or apps/iam/apply.sh) under the deploy lock …
<the policy: the accepted skeleton with the applied version's matrix and waivers>

entries_digest is the applied version's; skeleton_digest the accepted skeleton's, checked against the body; body_digest covers the matrix and waivers; groups and roles count the version's keys and keys is their digest; dropped counts rows the skeleton no longer has a group or role for. A rewrite happens only when the version, kind, a digest or the second line differs, or the body differs from the database; a new written_at or commit alone is not a difference. state/seed-facts.jsonl (account facts waiting for the history), state/break-glass/ (break-glass copies) and state/retired-<time>-<why>/ (files moved aside) live next to it.

Policy commands of slices 4.2a and 4.4

CommandWhat it does
./apps/iam/apply.sh --bootstrap | --resume --by <username> [--check-only], --statusSee apply.sh. Exit 0 done, 1 refused (nothing changed), 2 usage or a precondition, 3 committed but not finished (--resume).
./apps/iam/apply.sh --propose-skeleton --by <username> --reason "<text>" --from-git <rev> [--check-only]Since slice 4.4: the skeleton of commit <rev>, which must widen access and pass the checker with the applied matrix, becomes a submitted version of kind skeleton with its diff, for qa's approval. Refused under break-glass, while another change or skeleton version is open, or while an apply is unfinished. Exit 0 done (or would do, --check-only), 1 refused (nothing changed), 2 usage or a precondition (no --from-git, no applied version, an unknown --by). See apply.sh.
./apps/iam/apply.sh --withdraw-open --by <username> --reason "<text>" [--check-only]Since slice 4.4: withdraws the open change or skeleton version as system:apply, without Keycloak. Exit 0 done or nothing open, 1 refused, 2 usage.
cli.py skeleton-gate --state F [--check], --against-git REVCompares the git skeleton with the accepted one (from a state document, or from defaults.yaml at a git revision) and prints equal, narrowing or widening with what each role gains (and, for the network rules, database role names, MQTT logins and any key the gate does not know, "section <key> changed"). Exit 0, or 3 on a widening; with --state, 2 and verdict refused when a narrowing with the applied matrix fails the checker; writes nothing.
cli.py write-applied --state F [--if-different]Writes the host file from a state document, under the deploy lock (the deploys and apply.sh call it).
cli.py export-appliedPrints defaults.yaml with only its matrix: and waivers: replaced by the applied version's, for an occasional commit; writes nothing. Exit 2 without a host file.
cli.py bootstrap-targets --out DIR [--source host]Compiles every policy output from defaults.yaml, from version 1 and from a simulated host file (or the written one) and compares them byte for byte; apply.sh runs it.
apps/iam/apply_check.pyThe offline parts of apply.sh and of the health check: can-approve, accounts, by-user, seed-fact, seed-build, seed-queue, simulate-state, bundle-sql, apply-record, bootstrap-targets, health, status, owners, and since slice 4.4 propose (the skeleton proposal: gate, checker, open version, diff and its SQL), open-version, withdraw-sql and maker-warn. It reads JSON and prints text; it never holds a secret.

Settings

The settings an administrator may change in config.env, with the default from config.env.example. Change them with ./infra/configure.sh or, for the IAM switches, with ./guide.sh, which runs the deploys each switch needs in the right order. A changed setting takes effect at the next deploy of the parts that read it.

Identity and network

NameDefaultMeaning
PROJECT_NAMEmedceaThe MQTT topic root, the Kubernetes namespace and the Keycloak realm. Lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, starting with a letter. Changing it after boards are flashed means reflashing them.
NAMESPACE$PROJECT_NAMEThe Kubernetes namespace.
K8S_CHANNEL1.29/stableMicroK8s Kubernetes version.
PLATFORM_IPthis machine's Tailscale IPv4 address, else its first LAN addressThe address every service name points to.
DOMAIN${PLATFORM_IP}.nip.ioBase domain: every service is <name>.$DOMAIN. Can be IP-based (no DNS setup), a short name the platform answers DNS for, or your own DNS domain.
LOCAL_DNSfalsetrue: the platform runs a DNS server for $DOMAIN on port 53 and maps the names in this machine's /etc/hosts.
ALIAS_DOMAIN${PLATFORM_IP}.nip.ioA previous domain kept as permanent redirects to $DOMAIN, so old links still work. Inactive when equal to DOMAIN or empty.
USE_TAILSCALEemptytrue: a deploy stops if Tailscale is not up. false: LAN only. Empty: not decided yet.
*_DOMAINsee Web addressesThe address of each application.

First accounts

NameDefaultMeaning
ADMIN_USER, ADMIN_EMAILadmin, emptyThe platform administrator. The e-mail address receives Keycloak password resets and is the login of pgAdmin's built-in user (used only with pgAdmin's break-glass sign-in); when Keycloak cannot be read during ./deploy-infra.sh and pgAdmin has no list of its own yet, it is the one address that gets pgAdmin's IoT Postgres entry (otherwise pgAdmin keeps its current list).
QA_USER, QA_EMAILemptyThe first person with role qa, who confirms account grants and approves policy changes. Must be someone other than the administrator.
ENGINEER_USER, ENGINEER_EMAILemptyThe first person with role engineer, the only role admitted to Node-RED and MQTT Explorer; device onboarding admits it and, into existing slots only, maintenance. May be a second account of the administrator, with its own username and e-mail address.
KC_BREAKGLASS_USERkc-breakglassKeycloak's own console account (realm master), used only for recovery. Every use is recorded in Keycloak's admin events.
GRAFANA_ADMIN_USER, GRAFANA_DEVELOPER_USER, GRAFANA_VIEWER_USERadmin, developer, viewerThe demo accounts from before IAM Phase 3.1. Used only while IAM_GATES is false (the rollback), which creates them with the old Grafana roles. Otherwise nothing creates them, and accounts that already exist are left as they are.

IAM switches

Change these with ./guide.sh (IAM switches), which checks each switch's preconditions and runs its enable or rollback steps. After a fresh install, IAM switches → Bring IAM to full enforcement turns on every switch still false, in order (see After a fresh install).

NameDefaultMeaning
IAM_GATEStrueEach signed-in host admits the roles compiled from the access policy. false falls back to the pre-policy list (operator, qa, auditor, admin), and every deploy prints a warning.
IAM_NETPOLtrueOne network policy per application pod, so only its sign-in proxy and the listed services can connect to it. false removes them.
IAM_ENFORCEfalsefalse (report-only): every service checks each request against the route table but only logs "would refuse". true: the services answer 401, 403 or 503 to what the route table refuses. The administration side of the User Manager and Database Management refuse in both modes. Takes effect about a minute after ./deploy-infra.sh, without a restart.
IAM_POSTGREST_JWTfalsetrue: the configuration API verifies each person's sign-in token, so writes are attributed to the person and an expired or forged token is refused.
IAM_DB_SERVICE_ROLESfalsetrue: each service connects to the database under its own role instead of the database superuser. pgAdmin then has limited rights: it can no longer change table structure or write data.
IAM_DB_ROLESfalsetrue: the database itself enforces each role's rights, limits operators to their own units and refuses changes to locked records. Refused until IAM_POSTGREST_JWT and IAM_DB_SERVICE_ROLES are on. Operators need units assigned first (apps/iam/units.sh).
IAM_MQTT_PERSONfalsetrue: the MQTT Explorer connects with a short-lived personal credential made from the person's role, accepted only on the broker's WebSocket listener, false: one sign-in check for both listeners, and the Explorer has no credential (its page says it needs true); the shared mqtt-explorer account was removed on 1 November 2026. Needs iam-api (./apps/iam/deploy.sh) and IAM_DB_ROLES=true.

Changes of IAM_DB_ROLES are recorded in the access policy history; so are changes of IAM_MQTT_PERSON, by ./deploy-infra.sh, and every MQTT Explorer credential issued. When ./deploy-infra.sh applies a changed IAM_ENFORCE, it records an enforcement_enabled or enforcement_disabled event there.

Services and storage

NameDefaultMeaning
POSTGRES_USERpostgresDatabase superuser name.
POSTGRES_DBmedcea_dbDatabase name.
STORAGE_SIZE10GiDatabase volume size.
GRAFANA_STORAGE2GiSize of Grafana's volume (grafana: the dashboards Editors make in the browser, annotations, sessions; kept, and copied by the nightly backup). Read when the volume is created; an existing volume keeps its size.
BACKUP_DIR/var/backups/$PROJECT_NAMEThe host folder for the nightly backups, created mode 700 for the account that runs ./apps/backup/deploy.sh. It holds secrets. Another disk than the data's is better. No uninstall deletes it. See Backup and restore.
BACKUP_KEEP_DAYS14After a good run, dated backup folders older than this many days are deleted (never the newest complete one).
NODE_RED_TZEurope/LondonNode-RED time zone (its log, debug output and local times in flows). Applied by ./deploy-infra.sh.
KEYCLOAK_REALM$PROJECT_NAMEThe Keycloak realm that holds every platform account.
WORKBENCH_ENABLEDtruefalse skips the AI workbench in ./deploy-infra.sh (about 2 GB of images).
WORKBENCH_IMAGEquay.io/jupyter/scipy-notebook:latest@sha256:d416eea5…c2ceNotebook image for each workbench user, pinned by digest to the image verified on 31 October 2026: JupyterLab 4.6.4 with the Git panel (0.55) and nbdime. Change it only to another digest or a date tag; latest without a digest works but warns, because JupyterLab and its Git panel can change under people at any pull. An existing config.env keeps its value until you change it.
WORKBENCH_STORAGE10GiPersistent home size per workbench user (volume claim-<user>; kept, and copied by the nightly backup).
WORKBENCH_NOTEBOOK_REPOemptyThe research repository on GitHub, as OWNER/REPO (for example smvieira/medcea-notebooks, created by you), where scientists commit their notebooks with their own GitHub accounts. Shown to them in every workspace; empty means the guide tells them to ask you. Not a credential. Takes effect at ./infra/deploy-workbench.sh, for workspaces started afterwards.
EDGE_WIZARD_PORT8088Port of the onboarding wizard on the host. Must match the redirect addresses registered in Keycloak.
EDGE_WIZARD_ROLEemptyUsed only with IAM_GATES=false: the role a person must hold to provision boards; empty means any signed-in account. With IAM_GATES=true the access policy decides and this value is ignored with a warning.
DOCKERHUB_USERemptyDocker Hub account, for chart pulls and to avoid pull limits.

Secrets

config.env also holds passwords, client secrets and cookie secrets. ./infra/configure.sh asks you for three of them: ADMIN_PASS, POSTGRES_PASS and the Docker Hub access token DOCKERHUB_PAT. The others are generated by ./infra/configure.sh or ./deploy-infra.sh and must not be edited by hand: the initial passwords QA_INITIAL_PASS and ENGINEER_INITIAL_PASS (replaced at first sign-in), KC_BREAKGLASS_PASS, GRAFANA_BREAKGLASS_PASS (Grafana's local admin), KC_DEPLOYER_SECRET, one Keycloak client secret and one cookie secret per signed-in host, the database role passwords (SVC_*_DB_PASS, IAM_SVC_DB_PASS, RR_ADMIN_LOGIN_DB_PASS, AUTHENTICATOR_DB_PASS) and the MQTT service passwords (MQTT_*_PASS). MQTT_EXPLORER_PASS, the password of the MQTT Explorer's removed shared account, is no longer used: if an older config.env still has it, ./infra/configure.sh says so in one line and it is ignored; you can delete the line.

On the cluster, secrets are kept in Kubernetes Secrets, never as plain values in a deployment, which anyone allowed to read deployments could see. pgAdmin's client secret and its first-start password (the platform admin's, ADMIN_PASS) are in the Secret pgadmin-config since 1 November 2026; before that they were plain values in the pgAdmin deployment. Kubernetes also keeps older copies of a deployment (ReplicaSets) that anyone allowed to read deployments may read too, so pgAdmin's deployment keeps none. If that change is ever reverted, ./deploy-infra.sh puts the plain values back and leaves the Secret unused; it does no harm and can be deleted by hand.

config.env and its backups (config.env.guide-<timestamp>.bak) hold every password. Keep them on the host only. E-mail settings are not in config.env: set them in the User Manager under E-mail settings.

Scripts and commands

Run every script from the repository root on the platform host. Scripts that change the cluster or config.env take the deploy lock, so only one runs at a time; a second one stops and names the run that holds the lock.

CommandWhat it doesTakes the lock
./install.shInstalls or updates everything: infrastructure, then applications, then a health check. --uninstall-apps, --uninstall-infra and --uninstall-all remove parts of the platform. --break-glass "<reason>" (with MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE exported) is policy break-glass for the whole install, asked once (see Policy break-glass).yes
./guide.shQuestion-and-answer guide to every command below: shows the exact command and runs it on confirmation. Also the way to turn the IAM switches on and off, one at a time or, with Bring IAM to full enforcement, every switch still false in order. --dry-run runs nothing.for a switch recipe, once for all its steps (for Bring IAM to full enforcement, once for the whole chain); other commands take their own
./deploy-infra.shDeploys the infrastructure: MicroK8s, PostgreSQL, Keycloak and its clients, Node-RED, Grafana, pgAdmin, the portal, the AI workbench. Publishes the IAM settings to the services. --configure reviews settings; --reset wipes the namespace; --uninstall removes MicroK8s; --policy-only re-renders only the policy artefacts that drifted (see Re-rendering only what drifted). Any other option is refused.yes
./infra/configure.shFills config.env by question and answer. --missing asks only for required values that are empty.yes
./infra/bootstrap-infra.shSets up MicroK8s. Called by ./deploy-infra.sh.yes
./infra/deploy-workbench.shDeploys or redeploys only the AI workbench (JupyterHub). --policy-only: only its gate and the notebooks' hops, and only when they drifted.yes
./infra/publish-manual.shBuilds this manual from its chapters and publishes it to the portal at /manual/, nothing else (see the manual in the portal). ./deploy-infra.sh does it too.yes
./deploy-apps.shDeploys application components: a menu, or --all. --uninstall [--purge-data] [component ...] removes them (--purge-data also moves the host's policy files aside). --break-glass "<reason>" (with MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE exported) is policy break-glass for the selected components, asked once; without it an exported MEDCEA_POLICY_FILE is ignored.yes
./apps/<component>/deploy.shDeploys one component: access, database, edge-wizard, frontend, iam, monitor, mqtt-explorer, node-red, report (Grafana dashboards), scheduler, backup (the nightly backup). Each has an uninstall.sh. --gate-only (access, edge-wizard, frontend, iam, monitor, mqtt-explorer, scheduler): only the sign-in gate, and only when it drifted from the applied policy (see Re-rendering only what drifted). Any other option is refused.yes
./apps/iam/deploy.shChecks the access policy against its invariants and reports which roles each host admits; while no policy version exists, bootstraps version 1 (apply.sh --bootstrap --by MEDCEA_POLICY_BOOTSTRAP_BY, else ADMIN_USER) and finishes an interrupted apply; then deploys iam-api.yes
./apps/iam/apply.shPolicy versions: --bootstrap, --resume, --status (see apply.sh).yes, but not --status or --check-only
apps/iam/units.sh assign | remove | listAssigns units to an operator or removes them. An assignment waits for a qa person to confirm it in the User Manager; a removal applies at once. --by names you.yes
./apps/backup/backups.sh now | list | verify | restore-helpRuns the nightly backup once now and waits for it; lists the backup folders; checks a folder against its checksums; prints the restore steps for a folder (runs none of them).now only
./health-check.shRuns every check in health-checks.conf and prints OK, WARN, FAIL or SKIP for each. -v shows detail.no
python3 apps/iam/compiler/cli.py hostsLists every host, whether its gate is compiled, and the roles it admits. cli.py check checks the policy invariants; its first line says where the policy was read from (state/policy-applied.yaml once version 1 exists, else defaults.yaml). cli.py export-applied prints the applied policy as defaults.yaml.no
./edge/flash-device.shFlashes one ESP32 board over USB, gives it a slot from the database and registers it. --ui serves the same steps as a web page.no
tests/iam-check.shChecks that every service runs the access check with the IAM_ENFORCE setting of config.env, and that each service marks a forged request as refused (in report-only mode it is logged as "would refuse" and let through).no
tests/iam-hosts.shChecks that each host's live gate admits exactly the roles the policy compiles.no
tests/iam-signin.shSigns in one throwaway account per role to every host and checks it gets in exactly where the policy says. Deletes the accounts on exit.yes
tests/iam-workbench.shChecks with two throwaway accounts that the AI workbench's administrator cannot open another person's workspace, that removing a person from the hub keeps their files, and that a removed role or a disabled account is refused at the next sign-in.yes
tests/iam-access.shTests account decisions in the User Manager with throwaway accounts.yes
tests/iam-network.shChecks that the network policies block what they should and nothing the platform needs.yes
tests/iam-mqtt.shChecks with throwaway accounts that the MQTT Explorer's personal credentials carry the policy's topics, work only on the WebSocket listener, and end when the account is deactivated (needs IAM_MQTT_PERSON=true). With --commissioning <UNIT> (opt-in) it also flags an idle unit for commissioning through the Configuration UI with a throwaway qa account and ends it again; its proposal and approval rows stay, since they are append-only.yes

./deploy-infra.sh --reset and --uninstall, ./install.sh --uninstall-infra, --uninstall-all and --uninstall-apps --purge-data, and ./deploy-apps.sh --uninstall --purge-data delete platform data. Each asks you to confirm before it removes anything. The first four also delete every AI workbench home folder; before they ask, they list each one with the directory that holds it and how to keep it (see Uninstalling).

Health checks

./health-check.sh runs the checks listed in health-checks.conf, 55 since 3 November 2026. Most check that a workload is ready, a web address answers or a port is open; the IAM checks read the database and the cluster without changing anything.

CheckWhat it checks
PostgreSQL, Keycloak, Mosquitto, Node-RED, Grafana, pgAdmin, MQTT Explorer, DB Mgmt, Run Monitor, Scheduler, User Manager, PortalThe workload is rolled out and ready.
OAuth2 Proxy (Node-RED), MQTT Explorer proxy, Config UI proxy, DB Mgmt proxy, Run Monitor proxy, Scheduler proxy, User Manager proxy, Portal OAuth2 ProxyThe sign-in proxy in front of the application is ready.
Node-RED UI, MQTT Explorer UI, Config UI, DB Mgmt UI, Run Monitor UI, Scheduler UI, User Manager UI, Portal UI, AI Workbench, Onboarding wizard UIThe address answers with a redirect (to the sign-in), which is the healthy answer for a signed-out request.
Keycloak HTTPSKeycloak answers over HTTPS.
Grafana UI, pgAdmin UIThe application's own health page answers.
Grafana rolesGrafana's role setting is the one compiled from the access policy, refuses roles without a setting, and cannot give Grafana Admin. FAIL on any difference (run ./deploy-infra.sh); WARN while IAM_GATES is false.
AI Workbench gateThe AI workbench admits exactly the roles compiled from the access policy, its administrator cannot open others' workspaces, removing a person from the hub keeps their files, and notebooks cannot reach private addresses except Keycloak and config-api, and those only while IAM_POSTGREST_JWT and IAM_DB_ROLES are true. FAIL on any difference (run ./infra/deploy-workbench.sh); WARN while IAM_GATES is false; SKIP while WORKBENCH_ENABLED is not true.
Node-RED flows vs gitThe flows Node-RED runs against the flows in the repository. FAIL when a repository node was changed or deleted, or no flows run (run ./apps/node-red/deploy.sh); WARN on flows or settings that exist only in the editor.
Node-RED editor rightsThe editor's own sign-in is on, with the rights compiled from the access policy, and its admin interface refuses a call without a sign-in. FAIL on any difference (run ./deploy-infra.sh).
BackupsThe newest complete backup in BACKUP_DIR (its OK marker) is at most 26 hours old. WARN beyond 26 hours, FAIL beyond 72; SKIP before ./apps/backup/deploy.sh, and on its first day. Run it as the account that deployed the backup, which owns the folder.
Platform manualThe manual the portal serves at /manual/ is the one the repository's chapters build now (a digest of a fresh build against the one published). WARN when it lags or is not published (./guide.sh → Infrastructure → Publish the manual to the portal), or the portal does not mount it; FAIL when the chapters do not build or the published copy is not the page it claims to be; SKIP before the portal exists.
Mosquitto MQTT (TCP)The broker port for boards (31883) is open.
Onboarding wizardThe wizard service on the host ($PLATFORM_IP:$EDGE_WIZARD_PORT) answers.
Onboarding rolesThe running wizard admits exactly the roles compiled from the access policy (engineer, maintenance). FAIL on any difference, or when it admits any signed-in account (run ./apps/edge-wizard/deploy.sh when no run is active). WARN while IAM_GATES is false. SKIP when the wizard is not running.
pgAdmin rolespgAdmin's sign-in admits exactly the roles compiled from the access policy (admin), pgAdmin can read its database password file, and every address in its server list owns the IoT Postgres entry. FAIL on any difference (run ./deploy-infra.sh). WARN while IAM_GATES is false, or while the server list is empty.
Access policy historyThe hash chain of the access policy history is intact (FAIL names the first broken event).
Database roles modeThe IAM_DB_ROLES mode recorded in the database matches config.env. WARN while it is false.
Database grantsThe database's rights equal those compiled from the access policy. FAIL on any difference.
Access policy versionA policy version is applied and state/policy-applied.yaml is exactly that version; the git skeleton adds no access over the accepted one, no earlier accept added any, the last apply finished, no policy break-glass is open, the version audit, the history's chain and the waivers' review dates are clean. WARN on an apply in progress, queued account facts, a narrowing git skeleton, git lagging the applied matrix, a waiver due within 30 days; SKIP before migration 008c.
Policy artefactsThe route table and MQTT rows in medcea-iam-lib and the roles each sign-in proxy admits are the applied version's compile; proxies not deployed are counted. FAIL on a difference (the message names the deploy); WARN while the User Manager's gate is admin-only; SKIP before ./deploy-infra.sh.
Account decisionsAccount decisions waiting for qa. WARN when one has waited over 7 days or a mail settings change is waiting.
Changes waiting for qaProposed configuration changes (unit commissioning, a log setting, the review of a commissioning end) waiting for qa. WARN when one has waited over 7 days, with its number (Configuration UI, Approvals tab); SKIP before the database deploy that adds the approval functions.
Database clientsWhich database role each service connects as, against IAM_DB_SERVICE_ROLES.
PostgREST tokensThe configuration API verifies tokens as IAM_POSTGREST_JWT says, its signing keys are current, and a forged token is refused with 401. WARN while the switch is false.
Service enforcementThe services' IAM_ENFORCE setting matches config.env, and every pod's own copy agrees. WARN while it is false; FAIL if it is true while IAM_DB_ROLES is false, or while a pod's own copy still says false.
MQTT person credentialsThe broker, the MQTT Explorer page and iam-api follow IAM_MQTT_PERSON, and every credential is on its own listener. WARN while it is false; FAIL on any disagreement.

Glossary

Abbreviations and terms used in this manual.

TermMeaning
ACLAccess control list. The broker's list of which topics each MQTT account may read or write.
ALCOA+Data integrity principles: attributable, legible, contemporaneous, original, accurate, plus complete, consistent, enduring and available.
Annex 11EU GMP Annex 11, the rules for computerised systems.
Break-glassEmergency access outside the normal rules, recorded when used: the Keycloak console account and the administrator's access to the host.
CACertificate authority. The platform's own CA ("CEA Platform CA") signs the HTTPS certificates of its addresses.
CEAControlled environment agriculture. MedCEA is medicinal CEA.
EBRElectronic batch record. Planned, not implemented yet.
EPCISGS1 Electronic Product Code Information Services, a standard for traceability events. Planned, not implemented yet.
ESP32The microcontroller boards used as sensor and control devices.
GACPGood agricultural and collection practice, for medicinal plants.
GMPGood manufacturing practice.
GxPThe family of good practice rules (GMP, GACP and others).
IAMIdentity and access management: accounts, roles and the access policy.
JWTJSON Web Token. The signed sign-in token Keycloak issues and the services verify.
MHRAMedicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency (UK).
MQTTThe messaging protocol boards use to send telemetry and receive commands, through the Mosquitto broker.
OIDCOpenID Connect. The sign-in protocol between Keycloak and the applications.
PostgRESTThe service that exposes the database as a web API to the Configuration UI (the configuration API).
Q-tableThe learned state of a reinforcement-learning controller, kept as regulated data.
qaQuality Assurance role: approves configuration, account decisions and policy changes made by someone else.
qcQuality Control Analyst role: tests product. Defined for later use.
qpQualified or Responsible Person role: releases the output of a completed run in the Configuration UI's Approvals tab. No account on this install holds it yet.
RBACRole-based access control.
Report-onlyThe mode of IAM_ENFORCE="false": the services log what they would refuse but let the request through.
RLSRow-level security. A database rule that limits which rows a role sees, for example an operator's own units.
SSOSingle sign-on. One Keycloak sign-in opens every application your role admits.
TLSTransport Layer Security, the encryption behind HTTPS.
Unit, zoneA unit is a cultivation chamber; a zone is part of a unit. Each zone has one place for a sense board and one for a control board.

Revision history

This manual is updated at the end of every phase of the IAM plan. Each edition names the code it describes, so a reader can tell whether a page still matches the platform in front of them.

41 Nov 202673ffd31Release v0.4.0, the code for a fresh installation. New: the Configuration UI's Approvals tab (approve with a note, return, withdraw, history), the parameter-set editor and Retire in place of Delete; test mode (commissioning) of a unit, proposed by an engineer and approved by qa, refused while a run is waiting or running; release of a completed run by qp in the Approvals tab, with a Released badge in the Scheduler; a Training chapter of hands-on exercises; a log in logs/ for every deploy, health check and live test; sessions that follow the person signed in (the AI workbench, Grafana and the Node-RED editor re-check the platform sign-in within minutes; Sign out also ends the workbench); pgAdmin 9.19 with its secrets in a Kubernetes Secret and a ready server entry per administrator; the AI workbench on chart 4.4.4, its setup run before the server starts (notebooks did not open on a new home) and the prompt and git author showing the person; Grafana keeps its data; nightly backups of the databases and every data volume, with restore steps; the shared MQTT Explorer account is removed. A fresh-install review fixed a desktop-session restart during install, anonymous chart pulls, a workbench deploy timeout and the end of the full-enforcement chain. Records and "Last done" dates cited here belong to the install that ran until 1 November 2026.
331 Oct 2026cb84d72Phase 3 complete: Grafana, the AI Workbench, Node-RED, the onboarding wizard and pgAdmin take their rights from the access policy, and MQTT Explorer uses a personal credential per person (IAM_MQTT_PERSON). New: notebooks that read platform data with the person's own read-only sign-in, saving notebooks to git, who reaches production data and how, the test accounts on this install, "After a fresh install" and the guide action that turns every IAM switch on in order. Two defects found by the live tests and fixed the same day are recorded in the known gaps: a workbench administrator could reach another person's server, and Node-RED's sign-in proxy logged editor tokens.
231 Oct 2026b7cba75Phase 2 complete. IAM_ENFORCE turned on at 09:54 UTC: the services refuse what the access policy does not allow (401, 403 or 503) instead of only logging it. Access control chapter states the enforcing state; the decision record notes that the planned report-only trial was shortened, and why.
131 Oct 2026a9639b7First edition. Describes the platform as built through IAM phases 0, 1 and 2 up to step 2.6, deployed in report-only mode: all eleven roles offered, run logs compressed and never deleted, Q-tables retired rather than deleted, exports limited to the person's export groups and logged, operators limited to their assigned units. IAM_ENFORCE is still off at this edition.
Source: docs/manual/ in the MedCEA repository; built by docs/manual/build.py from one file per chapter. Updated at the end of every phase of the IAM plan.
